- **Extra file in the first commit:** `commit -a` also committed `documentation/ai-logs/README.md`. The only change there was the automatic log export adding a link to this morning's export file. That is correct, so I kept it.
- **Session log:** I committed this session's conversation log as `2c3404f` on `dev-features`, and pushed it to `main-features` too.
- **My notes:** I recorded the same branch rule in my memory for future sessions.

`dev-mcp` and `main-mcp` got only the `CLAUDE.md` change, not the conversation-log commits.

# "Claude only" mode (dev-features): no Ollama containers.
#
#   docker compose -f docker-compose.yml -f docker-compose.claude-only.yml up -d --build
#
# Answers come from Claude (set ANTHROPIC_API_KEY in .env). Embeddings run inside the backend
# with the same embeddinggemma model (fastembed/ONNX), downloaded once (~1.2 GB) into the
# backend-models volume, so retrieval results are identical to the default setup.
services:
  ollama:
    profiles: ["ollama"] # not started unless the profile is requested
  ollama-init:
    profiles: ["ollama"]
  backend:
    depends_on: !reset {}
    environment:

      ENABLED_LLM_PROVIDERS: anthropic

### 🤖 Claude

Found issue: uvicorn `--reload` binds port before it listens, so summary printed too early (first proxy call refused). Readiness must use real connect, not bind.
<details>
<summary>🔧 Bash: Stop test run; use connect-based readiness</summary>

### 🤖 Claude

Tighten stop: kill whole group even if group leader already exited (Vite could outlive npm).
<details>
<summary>🔧 Edit: /home/sako/git/ep_kb_rag_chatbot/scripts/start.py</summary>

```
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/sako/git/ep_kb_rag_chatbot/scripts/start.py",
  "old_string": "def stop(procs: list[subprocess.Popen[bytes]]) -> None:\n    for p in procs:\n        if p.poll() is None:\n            if os.name == \"nt\":\n                p.terminate()\n            else:  # the whole group: npm starts Vite through a shell that doesn't pass signals on\n                os.killpg(p.pid, signal.SIGTERM)\n    for p in procs:\n        try:\n            p.wait(timeout=10)\n        except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:\n            if os.name == \"nt\":\n                p.kill()\n            else:\n                os.killpg(p.pid, signal.SIGKILL)",
  "new_string": "def signal_group(p: subprocess.Popen[bytes], sig: signal.Signals) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Signal the process and, on POSIX, its whole group: npm starts Vite through a shell\n    that doesn't pass signals on, and Vite may outlive npm.\"\"\"\n    if os.name == \"nt\":\n        if p.poll() is None:\n            p.kill() if sig == signal.SIGKILL else p.terminate()\n        return\n    try:\n        os.killpg(p.pid, sig)\n    except ProcessLookupError:\n        pass  # the group is already gone\n\n\ndef stop(procs: list[subprocess.Popen[bytes]]) -> None:\n    for p in procs:\n        signal_group(p, signal.SIGTERM)\n    for p in procs:\n        try:\n            p.wait(timeout=10)\n        except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:\n            signal_group(p, signal.SIGKILL)"
}

### 🤖 Claude

A busy port no longer stops the chatbot from starting. Start it with `python3 scripts/start.py dev` or `python3 scripts/start.py docker`. If a preferred port is in use, it moves to the next free one and prints the addresses in use (this is how Vite already handles its own port).

| Command | Preferred ports | Change them in `.env` with |
|---|---|---|
| `python3 scripts/start.py dev` (backend + frontend on your machine) | backend 8000, frontend 5173 | `BACKEND_PORT`, `FRONTEND_DEV_PORT` |
| `python3 scripts/start.py docker [--claude-only]` | web UI 8080, Ollama 11434 | `FRONTEND_PORT`, `OLLAMA_PORT` |

What you see when ports are taken:

```
Port check:
  ! Backend: port 8000 is already in use, using 8001 instead
  ! Frontend: port 5173 is already in use, using 5174 instead
========================================================================
  OmniCorp KB chatbot is running (local development)
  Chat UI      http://localhost:5174
  Admin area   http://localhost:5174/admin
  Backend API  http://localhost:8001/api/v1/health
  Swagger UI   http://localhost:8001/docs
  Ollama       http://localhost:11434
  CLI          cd backend && uv run python -m app.cli --url http://localhost:8001
  Note: Backend uses port 8001 because 8000 is already in use.
  Note: Frontend uses port 5174 because 5173 is already in use.
```

**How it behaves:**
- **Summary timing:** the addresses are printed only once the servers actually accept connections.
- **Frontend to backend:** the frontend's `/api` forwarding follows the backend to its new port.
- **Your own stack:** in Docker mode, a port already held by this project's running containers doesn't count as busy. Once 8080 is free again, the next start moves back to it.
- **Ollama port:** it can now be changed. `docker-compose.yml` reads `OLLAMA_PORT`, default 11434.
- **Stopping:** Ctrl+C stops both dev servers.
- **Plain `docker compose up`** doesn't check ports; it uses the values in `.env` as before.

**What I checked on the real setup:**
- **Dev mode:** with 8000 and 5173 taken, the servers came up on 8001 and 5174, and the chat page reached the backend. After stopping, no server process was left behind.
- **Docker mode, own containers:** with this project's containers already running, the stack stayed on 8080 and 11434.
- **Docker mode, 8080 taken:** with another program on 8080, the web UI moved to 8081 and printed that. When 8080 was free again, the next start moved it back.

Three problems came up in those runs and are fixed:
- The summary was printed before the backend could answer.
- A port was sometimes wrongly reported as busy just after an earlier connection to it had closed.
- Stopping left the Vite dev server running.

Tests pass: 166 backend (7 of them new, for the port logic) and 35 frontend. It's on `dev-features` and `dev-mcp`, CI passed on both, and `main-features` and `main-mcp` now match them. The README (new "Ports" section), the features guide troubleshooting table, `CLAUDE.md` and `.env.example` are updated.

`main` and `dev` (the official submission) don't have it. Should I add it there too?

---

### 🤖 Claude

The launcher already used the default ports whenever they were free, so the only code change is that it now says so. When no port is busy, the summary ends with a line like this:

```
Ports: Backend 8000, Frontend 5173 (the configured ports; none was busy)
```

If a port is busy, only that port moves to the next free one, and a note names it.

Checked by running it:
- **Dev mode, nothing blocking:** it used 8000 and 5173, the chat page reached the backend, and no "Port check" message appeared.
- **Docker mode:** it used 8080 and 11434 and printed the same kind of line. The running containers of this project hold those ports themselves, so they don't count as busy.
- **Busy ports** (tested before): 8000 moved to 8001, 5173 to 5174, and 8080 to 8081. Once 8080 was free again, the next start went back to it.

Ports set in `.env` (`FRONTEND_PORT` and the others) count as the defaults. Running `uvicorn`, `npm run dev` or `docker compose up` directly still uses the default ports without any check.

The change is on `dev-features` and `dev-mcp`, and the README "Ports" section now says that free default ports are always used. CI passed on both branches. On `dev-mcp` one timing test failed at first: API latency was 865 ms against a 500 ms limit. It passed when I re-ran it; this change doesn't touch the API. `main-features` and `main-mcp` now match their dev branches.

The [Model Context Protocol](https://modelcontextprotocol.io) lets AI assistants such as Claude Code, Claude Desktop or any MCP client use external tools. On `dev-mcp` the knowledge base is such a tool. An assistant can ask it questions and get the same cited, documentation-only answers as the chat, or read the sections and write its own answer.

### 6.1 What the server offers

| Name | Kind | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| `ask_knowledge_base(question, model?, language?)` | tool | The full pipeline: a cited answer, or `"refused": true` when the documentation has no answer. `model` is `anthropic` or `ollama`; `language` is an ISO code (`de`, `fr`, …) |
| `search_knowledge_base(query, k?)` | tool | The `k` best-matching sections (document, section, full text, similarity), for the assistant to answer from itself |
| `kb://documents` | resource | The list of articles |
| `kb://documents/{doc_id}` | resource | One article in full (Markdown) |

There are two ways to connect, and both give the same tools.

| | HTTP (in the running stack) | stdio (local subprocess) |
|---|---|---|
| Runs in | the backend container, at `/api/mcp/` | a process the clien

"""Runs the knowledge base as a local MCP server over stdio.

MCP clients (Claude Desktop, Claude Code, ...) start it as a subprocess, for example:

  claude mcp add omnicorp-kb -- uv run --directory /path/to/backend python -m app.mcp

It builds the same services as the API in-process, so Ollama must be reachable for the
embeddings (OLLAMA_BASE_URL) and ANTHROPIC_API_KEY is needed for Claude answers.
"""

import asyncio
import sys

from app.core.config import get_settings
from app.core.logging import configure_logging
from app.mcp.server import build_mcp_server
from app.services import create_services


async def serve() -> None:
    settings = get_settings()
    services = await create_services(settings)
    try:
        await build_mcp_server(lambda: services).run_stdio_async()
    finally:
        await services.aclose()


if __name__ == "__main__":
    configure_logging(get_settings().log_level, stream=sys.stderr)
```

### 🤖 Claude

I added the knowledge-base MCP server to Claude Desktop on this machine as `omnicorp-kb`, next to your `artemis` server. Claude Desktop isn't running right now, so it will appear the next time you start it; if it is open, restart it.

**How it's set up**
- **Separate checkout:** the MCP code exists only on `main-mcp`/`dev-mcp`, and your repo here is on `dev-features`. So I made a second checkout of `main-mcp` at `~/git/ep_kb_rag_chatbot-mcp` (a git worktree). Claude Desktop starts the server from there, so it keeps working whichever branch you check out in the main repo.
- **Settings:** that folder's `.env` is a link to your main repo's `.env`, so it uses the same Claude key and settings. I also copied the prepared search index there, so the first start doesn't rebuild it.
- **Desktop config:** the entry in `~/.config/Claude/claude_desktop_config.json` is:
  ```json
  "omnicorp-kb": {
    "command": "/home/sako/.local/bin/uv",
    "args": ["run", "--directory", "/home/sako/git/ep_kb_rag_chatbot-mcp/backend", "python", "-m", "app.mcp"],
    "type": "stdio"
  }
  ```
  I backed up the old config as `claude_desktop_config.json.bak-2026-09-27`.

**Checked:** I started the server the same way Claude Desktop will and connected an MCP client to it:
- It offered the two tools, `search_knowledge_base` and `ask_knowledge_base`.
- A search for "webhook signature" returned the *Webhooks › Verifying signatures* section.
- Asked with Claude, "How long are backups kept?" came back as "35 days [1]", with a citation.

**To use it:**
- **Ollama:** it must be reachable on `localhost:11434` for the search, so keep the Docker stack running.
- **Try it:** ask Claude Desktop something like *"Use omnicorp-kb: what is the first-response time for a P1 ticket on Enterprise?"*
- **Not in History:** questions asked through Claude Desktop don't show up in the admin History tab.
- **Updates:** when `main-mcp` changes, that folder needs updating too, and Claude Desktop needs a restart. I've noted this and will do it after future MCP changes.

**Other things done:**
- **Your `/export` file:** I checked it for keys (none found), moved it to `documentation/ai-logs/cli-exports/`, and committed and pushed it with the log update (`a456463` on `dev-features`).
- **Untracked `logs/` folder:** a new `logs/` folder in the repo root holds an identical copy of that export, made at 10:29. I didn't create it, so I left it alone; tell me if it should be deleted.
- **Local `main-*` branches:** your local copies of `main-features` and `main-mcp` were behind GitHub. I brought both up to date.

### 🤖 Claude

I couldn't fix the file on your laptop: this session runs on the VM, and the VM can't log in to your laptop over SSH (permission denied). What I can do is give you the entry to add there yourself.

**What was wrong**
- **Wrong machine:** this session runs on a KVM virtual machine (`test-environment`, 192.168.0.222). You connect to it from 192.168.0.81, which I take to be your laptop (`sako-Latitude-7280`). Claude Desktop isn't installed on the VM.
- **Wrong file:** the entry I added last time went into the VM's `~/.config/Claude/claude_desktop_config.json`, which no Claude Desktop reads. I've put that file back as it was (only `artemis`).
- **Why it can't just run on the laptop:** the chatbot code and Ollama are on the VM. So the laptop's Claude Desktop should start the knowledge-base server on the VM over SSH. Your laptop's key is already in the VM's `authorized_keys`, and I tested this exact command on the VM with a stripped-down environment like SSH gives. It listed the two tools, and a search returned the *First-response targets* section.

**On your laptop:**

1. Check that SSH works without a password prompt, because Claude Desktop can't answer one:
   ```bash
   ssh -T -o BatchMode=yes sako@192.168.0.222 'echo ok'
   ```
   It must print `ok`. If your key has a passphrase, load it into the SSH agent first.

2. Add the entry. This keeps your existing servers and saves a backup of the file first:
   ```bash
   python3 - <<'EOF'
   import json, pathlib, shutil
   p = pathlib.Path.home() / ".config/Claude/claude_desktop_config.json"
   cfg = json.loads(p.read_text()) if p.exists() else {}
   if p.exists():
       shutil.copy(p, p.with_name(p.name + ".bak"))
   cfg.setdefault("mcpServers", {})["omnicorp-kb"] = {
       "command": "ssh",
       "args": ["-T", "-o", "BatchMode=yes", "-o", "ConnectTimeout=10", "-o", "ServerAliveInterval=30",
                "sako@192.168.0.222",
                "/home/sako/.local/bin/uv run --quiet --directory /home/sako/git/ep_kb_rag_chatbot-mcp/backend python -m app.mcp"],
   }
   p.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   p.write_text(json.dumps(cfg, indent=2) + "\n")
   print("servers:", list(cfg["mcpServers"]))
   EOF
   ```
   If you'd rather edit it in vim, add this block inside `"mcpServers"`:
   ```json
   "omnicorp-kb": {
     "command": "ssh",
     "args": ["-T", "-o", "BatchMode=yes", "-o", "ConnectTimeout=10", "-o", "ServerAliveInterval=30",
              "sako@192.168.0.222",
              "/home/sako/.local/bin/uv run --quiet --directory /home/sako/git/ep_kb_rag_chatbot-mcp/backend python -m app.mcp"]
   }
   ```

3. Quit Claude Desktop completely and start it again. `omnicorp-kb` should then appear in its tools. To try it, ask: *"Use omnicorp-kb: what is the first-response time for a P1 ticket on Enterprise?"*

**Keep in mind:**
- **VM running:** the VM and its Docker stack must be running, because Ollama on the VM does the search.
- **Local `artemis`:** if your laptop's config has its own `artemis` entry, its paths must exist on the laptop. The one in the VM's file points to `/home/sako/git/artemis` on the VM.
- **IP address:** if the VM's address changes, update `192.168.0.222` in the entry.

If the server doesn't start, send me the contents of `~/.config/Claude/logs/mcp-server-omnicorp-kb.log` from the laptop.

### 🤖 Claude

I still can't change your laptop's config myself: this session runs on the VM, and the VM can't log in to your laptop. So I wrote a script that does the whole fix, and you start it with one command.

**Run this in a terminal on your laptop:**

```bash
ssh sako@192.168.0.222 cat .local/share/omnicorp-kb/install_claude_desktop_mcp.py | python3 -
```

The script is stored on the VM and runs on your laptop. It:
1. Checks that the laptop can log in to the VM without a password prompt. Claude Desktop can't type a password, so if this fails the script stops before changing anything and tells you what to do.
2. Backs up `~/.config/Claude/claude_desktop_config.json` to a `.bak-<date>` copy, adds `omnicorp-kb`, and keeps your other servers. It warns if another server points to a program that doesn't exist on the laptop.
3. Starts the knowledge-base server exactly as Claude Desktop will, over SSH, and lists its tools as a final check.

When it works, the output ends like this:
```
[ok] MCP handshake: server 'omnicorp-knowledge-base', tools: search_knowledge_base, ask_knowledge_base
```

Then quit Claude Desktop completely and start it again, and `omnicorp-kb` appears in its tools.

I ran the script on the VM against a test copy of the config file, piped in the same way. It added the entry, kept the existing server, warned about that server's missing program, and the final check listed both tools. I also tried a login that can't work: it stopped with a clear message and left the file unchanged. The part that talks to your laptop hasn't been run for real yet.

If you'd rather I do it directly next time, add the VM's public key to your laptop's `authorized_keys`. That gives this VM ongoing login access to your laptop, which the one-line command avoids.

### 🤖 Claude

Fixed. Claude Desktop on your laptop now starts `omnicorp-kb` without errors. Its log says `[omnicorp-kb] Server started and connected successfully`, and it has already loaded the tool list. The SSH login from the VM to the laptop worked this time, so I made the changes there directly.

**What was wrong**
- **Broken config file:** Desktop's `main.log` showed `Error reading or parsing config file (SyntaxError)`. The `omnicorp-kb` block had been pasted after the `"preferences"` section without a comma, and it wasn't inside a `"mcpServers"` section.
- **Wrong paths:** that block also pointed to `uv` and the `ep_kb_rag_chatbot-mcp` folder, which exist only on the VM, not on the laptop.

**What I did on the laptop**
1. **Rewrote the config** in `~/.config/Claude/claude_desktop_config.json`. I kept Desktop's own settings (the `preferences` section, all 17 entries) and added `omnicorp-kb` under `mcpServers`. The laptop's Claude Desktop now starts the server on the VM over SSH with your existing key. Backup of the broken file: `claude_desktop_config.json.bak-20260927-213007`.
2. **Tested the exact command Desktop uses.** It connected to the server, listed `search_knowledge_base` and `ask_knowledge_base`, and a real question came back from Claude: a P1 ticket on Enterprise gets a first response within **15 minutes**, with a citation.
3. **Started Claude Desktop again** in your desktop session, because it had been closed. Its logs show the server connected and no errors.

In Claude Desktop, try: *"Use omnicorp-kb: what is the first-response time for a P1 ticket on Enterprise?"*

**Keep in mind**
- **VM running:** the VM and its Docker stack must be running, because Ollama on the VM does the search.
- **IP address:** if the VM gets a new IP address, `192.168.0.222` in the entry needs updating.
- **VM login to the laptop:** the VM can now log in to your laptop over SSH. To remove that, delete the `sako@test-environment` line from `~/.ssh/authorized_keys` on the laptop.

### 🤖 Claude

Your Claude Desktop is connected right now: two copies of the server that Desktop started are running on the VM. It hasn't called any of the server's tools yet. You can check three things inside the app.

**1. Is the server connected?**
Open **Settings → Developer**. `omnicorp-kb` should be listed as **running**. If it failed, the error and a link to its log are shown there.

**2. Does the chat have its tools?**
In a new chat, open the tools menu next to the message box (the **+** or sliders icon, often labelled *Search and tools*). `omnicorp-kb` should be there with two tools, `search_knowledge_base` and `ask_knowledge_base`. Make sure it's switched on.

**3. Was it actually used for an answer?**
Ask something that only the knowledge base knows, for example:

> Use omnicorp-kb: what is the first-response time for a P1 ticket on Enterprise?

- The first time, Desktop asks for permission to use the tool. Choose **Allow once** or **Always allow**.
- Above the answer, a collapsible box appears, such as **"ask_knowledge_base (omnicorp-kb)"**. Expand it to see the question that was sent and the raw reply: `answer`, `citations` (for example `kb-002`) and `refused`.
- The answer should say **15 minutes**, with citations from the OmniCorp documents. Without the tool, Claude has no way to know this.

**On the laptop,** this command shows each tool call that Desktop makes:

```bash
grep "tools/call" ~/.config/Claude/logs/mcp-server-omnicorp-kb.log
```

The *Server started and connected successfully* lines are in `~/.config/Claude/logs/mcp.log`.

Questions asked from Claude Desktop don't appear in the chatbot's admin History tab. That tab only records the web chat, the API and the network version of the MCP server, not the version Desktop starts directly.

After you've asked a question, tell me and I'll check the log for the tool call.

You did ask it, and Claude Desktop did use the server; it just didn't show you an Allow prompt. The laptop's logs show the whole sequence:

- **21:34:03** – Desktop connected to `omnicorp-kb` and got its 2 tools.
- **21:39:10** – your 80-character question was sent in a local session (`local_45e07cb6…`), the kind Desktop uses for its Claude Code sessions.
- **21:39:26** – the server wrote a `chat_turn` entry for it:
  - `provider: ollama`, `model: ministral-3:3b`
  - `cited_docs: ["kb-005"]`, `refused: false`

So the call was `ask_knowledge_base` with no model named, which means the local model answered. It was not Claude.

**Why there was no Allow prompt:** that looks like a local Claude Code session inside Desktop. There, tool permission comes from the session's permission mode rather than a pop-up. Most likely your mode allows tools without asking, but I haven't confirmed that.

**How to see it in the app:** in that chat, the tool call appears as a collapsible entry above the answer, named `ask_knowledge_base` or `omnicorp-kb`. Expand it to see the question sent and the reply with its citations.

### 🤖 Claude

Yes. There are two ways that work today without any code change, and one small change I can make to the server.

**1. Let Claude pick the tool itself.** The tool is already switched on in that Claude Desktop session, and its description says it answers OmniCorp questions. So usually you can just ask:

> What is the first-response time for a P1 ticket on Enterprise?

Claude should call `omnicorp-kb` on its own. Adding "use the KB" at the end makes it reliable.

**2. Make it a standing instruction.** Your session is a local Claude Code session inside Claude Desktop, so it reads `CLAUDE.md` in the session's project folder on the laptop. You can also put the same text in the app's settings (Settings → Profile → personal preferences) so it applies to all chats. Add:

```
For any question about OmniCorp products, support, SLAs, security or integrations,
always answer with the omnicorp-kb tool ask_knowledge_base (model "ollama" unless I say "claude"),
and show its citations.
```

After that, plain questions go to the knowledge base, and "…with claude" switches the model.

**3. Server change I can make, about 30 minutes on `dev-mcp`:**
- **A short command:** add an MCP "prompt" to the server. It shows up as a slash command in the session, for example `/mcp__omnicorp-kb__ask What is the P1 response time? model=claude`. In the regular chat it appears in the **+** menu.
- **Model names:** make the tool accept the same names as the web chat and CLI (`claude`, `local`, `ministral`, `ministral-3:3b`) as well as `ollama` and `anthropic`. Right now `model ministral-3:3b` only works because Claude Desktop translates it.

I'd start with option 2, since it's instant and needs no code. Should I also build option 3?

"""Choosing the model by typing, as in the web chat: `/claude`, `/local`, `/ministral`, ...

The command names are the provider name, a short name and the model family. They are built
from `GET /providers`, so only the enabled models are offered.
"""

import re
from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Any

SHORT_NAMES = {"anthropic": "claude", "ollama": "local"}
# A command word; "/v3/records:batch ..." and other paths are questions, not commands.
COMMAND = re.compile(r"^/([a-z][a-z0-9.-]*)(?:\s+(.*))?$", re.IGNORECASE | re.DOTALL)
NAME = re.compile(r"^[a-z][a-z0-9.-]*$")


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ModelCommand:
    name: str
    provider: dict[str, Any]  # an entry of GET /providers


def model_commands(providers: list[dict[str, Any]]) -> dict[str, ModelCommand]:

````
### MCP server

The knowledge base is also an **MCP server**, so AI assistants such as Claude Code or Claude Desktop can use it directly:

| Tool / resource | What it does |
|---|---|
| `ask_knowledge_base(question, model?, language?)` | The full pipeline: a cited answer, or `refused: true` when the documentation has no answer |
| `search_knowledge_base(query, k?)` | The best-matching sections (document, section, text, score), for the assistant to answer from itself |
| `kb://documents` · `kb://documents/{doc_id}` | The list of articles, and one article in full |

**Over HTTP**, inside the running stack:
1. Set `MCP_TOKEN` in `.env` and restart the backend. Without it the endpoint doesn't exist.
2. The endpoint is `http://localhost:8080/api/mcp/`, and it expects `Authorization: Bearer <MCP_TOKEN>`.
3. It's served by the backend, so questions asked this way also appear in the admin history.

```bash
claude mcp add --transport http omnicorp-kb http://localhost:8080/api/mcp/ --header "Authorization: Bearer $MCP_TOKEN"
```

**Over stdio**, as a local subprocess:
- It needs Ollama reachable for the embeddings, and `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` for Claude answers. Its logs go to stderr.
- Claude Code:

  ```bash
  claude mcp add omnicorp-kb -- uv run --directory /path/to/repo/backend python -m app.mcp
  ```
- Claude Desktop (`claude_desktop_config.json`):

  ```json
  { "mcpServers": { "omnicorp-kb": { "command": "uv", "args": ["run", "--directory", "/path/to/repo/backend", "python", "-m", "
… [2105 more characters omitted]
````

### 6.1 What the server offers

| Name | Kind | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| `ask_knowledge_base(question, model?, language?)` | tool | The full pipeline: a cited answer, or `"refused": true` when the documentation has no answer. `model` is `anthropic` or `ollama`; `language` is an ISO code (`de`, `fr`, …) |
| `search_knowledge_base(query, k?)` | tool | The `k` best-matching sections (document, section, full text, similarity), for the assistant to answer from itself |
| `kb://documents` | resource | The list of articles |
| `kb://documents/{doc_id}` | resource | One article in full (Markdown) |

There are two ways to connect, and both give the same tools.

| | HTTP (in the running stack) | stdio (local subprocess) |
|---|---|---|
| Runs in | the backend container, at `/api/mcp/` | a process the client starts on your machine |
| Needs | `MCP_TOKEN` set; the stack running | `uv`, the repo, Ollama reachable, and optionally `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` |
| Security | Bearer token, and allowed host names | Local only, no network endpoint |
| History | Questions appear in the admin History and Statistics | Not recorded |
| Best for | Sharing one server; demos from any MCP client | A personal setup in Claude Code or Claude Desktop |

### 6.3 Set up over stdio

The client starts the server as a subprocess, which builds the same services in-process.

**Needs:**
- the repo and `uv` (`cd backend && uv sync`)
- Ollama reachable for the embeddings: the Docker stack publishes it on `127.0.0.1:114
… [3729 more characters omitted]

### 🤖 Claude

Finished. Your laptop's Claude Desktop now has the new version of the knowledge-base server. I restarted it, and it connected and loaded the tools and the new commands.

**How to ask now**
- **Commands:** in a local Code session in Claude Desktop (or in Claude Code), type the question after one of these:
  ```text
  /mcp__omnicorp-kb__ask What is the first-response time for a P1 ticket on Enterprise?
  /mcp__omnicorp-kb__ask_claude How long are backups kept?
  /mcp__omnicorp-kb__ask_local Which webhook events exist?
  ```
  `ask` uses the default model (the local one), `ask_claude` uses Claude, and `ask_local` uses the local model. Typing `/mcp__omnicorp` should suggest them. In a regular Claude Desktop chat they're in the **+** menu under `omnicorp-kb`.
- **Model names:** the knowledge-base tool now accepts the same model names as the chat and the command-line client: `claude`, `anthropic`, `local`, `ollama`, `ministral` and `ministral-3:3b`. An unknown name gets a list of the accepted ones. So *"Use omnicorp-kb with model ministral-3:3b: …"* now works directly, without Claude having to translate the name.
- **Plain questions** still work too.

**Testing**
- **Automated tests:** all 172 backend tests pass, including 2 new ones for the model names and the commands. CI passed on `dev-mcp`.
- **In the Claude Code app:** `/mcp__omnicorp-kb__ask_local What is the first-response time…?` sent the whole question to the knowledge base with the local model. The answer was **15 minutes**, citing kb-005.
- **In your Claude Desktop:** it connected to the new server and loaded the tools and commands. I haven't sent a question through your Desktop yet, so the first real question will be yours.

**`dev-mcp` is up to date with the current setup**
- **Code:** it already had everything from `dev-features`. The only differences are the MCP ones, as intended.
- **AI conversation logs:** these had been committed only on `dev-features`. I added them to `dev-mcp` in the same commit.
- **Desktop installer:** it is now in the repo as `scripts/install_claude_desktop_mcp.py`. It sets up Claude Desktop either on the same computer or, with `--ssh`, starting the server on another machine such as your VM, and checks the connection. I used it for your laptop. It replaced the entry, keeping Desktop's own settings, and saved a backup (`claude_desktop_config.json.bak-20260927-215614`).
- **Docs:** the README, the features guide (new section 6.5, "Asking questions from Claude Desktop and Claude Code"), `CLAUDE.md` and `ideas.md` describe all of this.
- **Branches:** `main-mcp` and the separate MCP checkout that Claude Desktop starts the server from (`~/git/ep_kb_rag_chatbot-mcp`) are updated.
- **This repo:** I switched it back to `dev-features`, where your Docker stack comes from.

The untracked `logs/` folder in the repo root is still there. I haven't touched it.

def main() -> None:
    script = Path(globals().get("__file__", "<stdin>"))
    piped = script.name == "<stdin>"  # `... | python3 -`: no repo next to the script
    repo_backend = None if piped else script.resolve().parent.parent / "backend"
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(
        description=__doc__, formatter_class=argparse.RawDescriptionHelpFormatter
    )
    parser.add_argument(
        "--ssh", metavar="USER@HOST", help="start the server on this machine over SSH"
    )
    parser.add_argument(
        "--backend", help="the repo's backend directory (on the SSH machine with --ssh)"
    )
    parser.add_argument("--name", default="omnicorp-kb", help="server name in Claude Desktop")
    parser.add_argument(
        "--config", default=str(default_config()), help="claude_desktop_config.json"
    )
    args = parser.parse_args()
    if args.backend is None:
        if args.ssh or repo_backend is None or not repo_backend.is_dir():
            parser.error("--backend is required with --ssh or when the script is piped in")
        args.backend = str(repo_backend)

    print(f"Adding '{args.name}' to Claude Desktop on {platform.node()}\n")
    check_server_machine(args)
    update_config(args)
    handshake(args)
    print("\nDone. Quit Claude Desktop completely and start it again.")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

### 🤖 Claude

Fixed. In Claude Desktop, use these short commands instead of `/mcp__omnicorp-kb__ask`:

```text
/kb What is the first-response time for a P1 ticket on Enterprise?
/kb-claude How long are backups kept?
/kb-local Which webhook events exist?
```

- `/kb` uses the default model (the local one), `/kb-claude` uses Claude, and `/kb-local` uses the local model.
- They should show up when you type `/` in a Code session in Claude Desktop.

**Why the message appeared:** Claude Desktop's Code sessions don't offer the knowledge-base server's commands (`/mcp__omnicorp-kb__ask`), so Desktop said *"isn't a command here"*. You still got an answer because Claude read your text as a normal question. Those Code sessions do list skills, which are small instruction files in `~/.claude/skills`, so I made the three commands as skills.

**What I did:**
- **The commands:** each one is a skill that passes your question unchanged to the knowledge-base tool with the right model. The reply shows the answer, the citations (document and section) and which model answered. If the documentation doesn't cover the question, Claude says so instead of guessing.
- **Tested** with the Claude Code app on the VM: `/kb-local What is the first-response time for a P1 ticket on Enterprise?` sent the whole question with the local model. The answer was **15 minutes**, citing kb-005.
- **Installer:** `scripts/install_claude_desktop_mcp.py` now installs these commands as well.
- **Your laptop:** I ran the installer there. It checked the connection to the VM, which listed the tools and commands, put the three commands in `~/.claude/skills`, and backed up the config first (`…bak-20260927-222107`). I then restarted Claude Desktop, and it reconnected to the server.
- **Repo:** committed to `dev-mcp` with the README, features guide (section 6.5) and `ideas.md` updated. CI passed. `main-mcp` and the MCP checkout Claude Desktop uses are updated, and this repo is back on `dev-features`.

The `/mcp__omnicorp-kb__ask…` commands still work in the Claude Code app and in the **+** menu of a regular Claude Desktop chat. I couldn't open the command list in Desktop's window myself. If `/kb` doesn't show up when you type `/`, open a new Code session so it loads the new commands.

### 🤖 Claude

The message came from the old command. The new `/kb` commands are loaded in your newest Claude Desktop session.

**What the logs on your laptop show**
- **Your last session:** you typed `/mcp__omnicorp-kb__ask what is the first-response time…`. That command doesn't exist in Claude Desktop, so Desktop showed *"isn't a command here"*. Claude then answered your text anyway with the knowledge base: 15 minutes, citing kb-005.
- **The same session:** `kb`, `kb-claude` and `kb-local` are in its list of available commands. So type:
  ```text
  /kb what is the first-response time for a P1 ticket on Enterprise?
  ```
  Older sessions, opened before the install, don't have these commands. Start a new session if `/kb` doesn't appear.
- **Keeping the old name as an alias:** that isn't possible. As far as I know, command names may only contain lowercase letters, digits and hyphens, and `mcp__omnicorp-kb__ask` has underscores.

**Adding the server to the model list**
- **Claude Desktop's model picker:** this can't be done. That list is fixed by Claude Desktop and only contains Claude models. The knowledge-base server is a tool Claude uses, not a model. Claude Desktop has no setting to add your own entries there.
- **Closest options in Claude Desktop:**
  1. **`/kb-claude` and `/kb-local`:** you already have them. They pick the knowledge base and its model in one step.
  2. **Tool toggle:** in a chat, the **+** / tools menu lets you switch `omnicorp-kb` on or off, per chat.
  3. **A Claude Desktop project, "OmniCorp KB":** its instructions say *"answer every question with omnicorp-kb"*. Every chat in that project then uses the knowledge base without a command. This comes closest to selecting it like a model.
- **The chatbot's own web page (http://localhost:8080):** it already has a model list (`claude`, `local`). Adding "MCP" there would add nothing, because the page and the server use the same answering code.

Did you mean Claude Desktop's model picker, or the chatbot web page's model list? If it's Claude Desktop, I can write the instructions for option 3 so you can paste them into a new project.

### 🤖 Claude

I wrote the Claude Desktop guide, pushed it to `dev-mcp` (CI passed), and opened pull request **#4 into `main-mcp`**: https://github.com/sarkiszhamakortsyan/ep_kb_rag_chatbot/pull/4

The guide is `documentation/mcp-claude-desktop.md`:
1. **How it works:** a diagram of Claude Desktop starting the server on the VM over SSH, what the server offers, and which model plays which part. It also explains that the server can't appear in Claude Desktop's model list.
2. **Setup:**
   - what you need, including checking that SSH works without a password prompt
   - a separate MCP checkout (`main-mcp` worktree) that keeps working when you switch branches
   - the install script, used from the same computer or over SSH, with its expected output
   - setting it up by hand for both cases, and Claude Code
3. **Usage:** `/kb`, `/kb-claude` and `/kb-local`, plain questions, the **+** menu in regular chats, a Claude Desktop project that always uses the knowledge base, and the permission prompt.
4. **Checking that it's used:** the tool step shown above the answer, Settings → Developer, and the log commands.
5. **Keeping it up to date** after `main-mcp` changes.
6. **Troubleshooting:** the problems we hit today, each with its cause and fix. These include the broken config file, *"isn't a command here"*, `CONNECTION_CLOSED`, SSH asking for a password, and unknown model names.

The README, the features guide (section 6) and `CLAUDE.md` link to it. The pull request also brings the conversation logs over from `dev-features`. It's a single commit, `e03d483`.

This pull request is the first change to reach `main-mcp` through review rather than a direct update. Your MCP checkout (`~/git/ep_kb_rag_chatbot-mcp`) stays on the current `main-mcp` until #4 is merged. It only adds documentation, so Claude Desktop keeps working either way.

The repo is back on `dev-features`, and the untracked `logs/` folder is still untouched.

<h1>Enterprise Knowledge Base RAG Chatbot</h1>

A prototype assistant for OmniCorp Solutions' Customer Success Managers (CSMs). It answers natural-language questions **strictly from internal documentation**, **cites the exact document sections** it used, and **refuses politely** (pointing to a human expert) when the documentation has no answer.

- **Backend:** Python 3.12 + FastAPI. Heading-aware chunking, exact in-memory vector search with an on-disk index cache, and a streaming RAG pipeline with citation mapping.
- **Frontend:** Vite + React + TypeScript + Tailwind. Streamed answers with progress steps and a Stop button, clickable `[n]` citation chips, source cards with a match level, copy and regenerate, light and dark themes.
- **LLM:** a local model via **Ollama** (`ministral-3:3b`, embeddings `embeddinggemma`), or **Claude** with your own API key (`claude-opus-5`), selectable per question.
- **One command:** `docker compose up`.

> The original assignment brief and the progress checklist are at the [end of this file](#assignment-brief).

## Extra features (dev-features branch)

Optional features from the [feature list](#features-which-we-can-try-to-implement), built phase by phase on top of the official version. The plan and progress are in [`ideas.md`](documentation/taskdocs/ideas.md#implementation-plan-2026-09-26).

> **How to use and test every feature, step by step:** [`documentation/features-guide.md`](documentation/features-guide.md). It includes a 10-minute demo script and troubleshooting.

### Admin area and response history

- **What it does:**
  - Every question and answer is stored in SQLite: text, sources, model, tokens and timings. The file lives in the `backend-db` Docker volume, so no extra service is needed.
  - The hidden admin page lists the stored turns with search and filters (model, answered or not covered, dates), shows a detail panel per answer, and exports CSV.
- **Open it:**
  1. Set `ADMIN_TOKEN` in `.env` to a long random value, for example `openssl rand -hex 24`.
  2. Restart the backend: `docker compose up -d backend`.
  3. Open `http://localhost:8080/admin`, or press **Ctrl+Shift+A** in the chat.
  4. Sign in with the token.
- **Security:** hiding the page isn't what protects it; the token is.
  - Without `ADMIN_TOKEN` the admin API doesn't exist (404).
  - A wrong or missing token gets 401, and the check uses a constant-time comparison.
  - The browser keeps the token only for the current tab (session storage).
- **Privacy:** questions can contain customer data.
  - Turns are de
… [9635 more characters omitted]

## Known limitations and future work

- **Single-turn questions (on `main`).** There each question is answered on its own, so a follow-up like *"and on the Enterprise plan?"* loses the topic. The dev-features branch rewrites follow-ups using the conversation (see *Follow-up questions*).
- **No authentication or rate limiting.** Fine for a local prototype. Before exposing it (especially with a Claude key), add auth such as SSO or an API gateway and per-user rate limits.
- **Ollama provides the embeddings** in the default setup, even when Claude answers, because Anthropic has no embeddings API. On th

30:- 3 that the knowledge base does **not** answer (pricing, LDAP, mobile offline mode)
42:`MIN_SCORE = 0.35` rejects off-topic questions without an LLM call and never rejects an answerable one. Near-topic unanswerable questions (LDAP 0.52, mobile 0.45) can't be separated by similarity alone, so the prompt makes the model refuse them (next table).
91:Tokens per answer: about 1,360 input (38% served from the prompt cache) and about 275 output. At the list prices for `claude-opus-5` ($5 / $25 per million input/output tokens, cache reads about 10% of the input price), that is roughly **$0.011 per question**. The whole 18-question benchmark costs about $0.20.
124:`gemma3:4b` missed q07, q11 and q12 by leaving out one detail each. Its fourth miss, q17, was the only **unsupported claim** in the whole comparison: *"OmniCorp does not integrate with on-premise LDAP"*, which the knowledge base doesn't say.

```
<h1>Enterprise Knowledge Base RAG Chatbot</h1>

A prototype assistant for OmniCorp Solutions' Customer Success Managers (CSMs). It answers natural-language questions **strictly from internal documentation**, **cites the exact document sections** it used, and **refuses politely** (pointing to a human expert) when the documentation has no answer.

- **Backend:** Python 3.12 + FastAPI. Heading-aware chunking, exact in-memory vector search with an on-disk index cache, and a streaming RAG pipeline with citation mapping.
- **Frontend:** Vite + React + TypeScript + Tailwind. Streamed answers with progress steps and a Stop button, clickable `[n]` citation chips, source cards with a match level, copy and regenerate, light and dark themes.
- **LLM:** a local model via **Ollama** (`ministral-3:3b`, embeddings `embeddinggemma`), or **Claude** with your own API key (`claude-opus-5`), selectable per question.
- **One command:** `docker compose up`.

> The original assignment brief and the progress checklist are at the [end of this file](#assignment-brief).

> **Branches.** This branch (`main`) is the submission for the assignment, and everything in this README describes it. Optional extensions built afterwards, such as an admin area, usage statistics, cost reports, a CLI and an MCP server, live on separate branches: see [Beyond the assignment](#beyond-the-assignment-optional-features).

---

- [Try it](#try-it)

## Architecture

```mermaid
flowchart LR

## API

Base path `/api/v1`. The OpenAPI schema is generated by FastAPI (`/docs` on the backend). Every error has the same shape:

```json
{ "error": { "code": "provider_unavailable", "message": "Ollama did not respond within 300 s ..." } }
```

| Method & path | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `POST /chat` | Answer a question and return the full result as JSON |
| `POST /chat/stream` | The same, as **Server-Sent Events**: `meta` → `token`… → `done`, or `error` |
| `GET /health` | Liveness (always 200) and readiness: `status` `starting`/`ok`/`degraded`, index stats, Ollama status |
| `GET /providers` | The enabled LLM providers, their models, the default, and whether each is usable now |
| `GET /admin/session` | Admin only (`Authorization: Bearer <ADMIN_TOKEN>`): checks the token |
| `GET /admin/history` | Admin only: stored questions and answers, newest first. Filters `q`, `provider`, `refused`, `from`, `to`; paging `limit`, `offset` |
| `GET /admin/history/{message_id}` | Admin only: one turn with the full answer, sources, tokens and timings |
| `GET /admin/history/export.csv` | Admin only: t

"""Runtime settings (ideas.md #7): switch model providers on/off and pick the default without a
restart. The configuration (.env) is the upper limit: only providers in ENABLED_LLM_PROVIDERS can
be enabled here. Changes are stored in SQLite and applied again at the next startup."""
import logging
from typing import Annotated, Any

from pydantic import BaseModel, ConfigDict, Field
from app.api.state import get_services
from app.api.v1.admin.common import ADMIN_ERRORS, History
from app.providers.errors import ProviderDisabledError
from app.services import Services
from app.stores.history.sqlite import SqliteHistory
router = APIRouter(prefix="/settings")
logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
MODEL_POLICY_KEY = "llm_providers"
class ProviderSetting(BaseModel):
    name: str
    model: str | None
    enabled: bool
    default: bool
class SettingsOut(BaseModel):
    providers: list[ProviderSetting]
    embedding_provider: str
    embedding_model: str
class SettingsIn(BaseModel):
    model_config = ConfigDict(extra="forbid")
    enabled_providers: list[str] = Field(min_length=1)
    default_provider: str
def apply_stored_policy(services: Services, history: SqliteHistory | None) -> None:
    """Re-applies the admin's last choice; ignored (with a warning) if .env no longer allows it."""
    stored = history.get_setting(MODEL_POLICY_KEY) if history else None
    if not stored:
        return
    try:

## Known limitations and future work

- **Follow-up questions** are rewritten from the last three turns (see [Follow-up questions](#follow-up-questions)). On `main`, each question is answered on its own.
- **No authentication or rate limiting.** Fine for a local prototype. Before exposing it (especially with a Claude key), add auth such as SSO or an API gateway and per-user rate limits.
- **Ollama provides the embeddings** in the default setup, even when Claude answers, because Anthropic has no embeddings API. The "Claude only" mode runs the same embedding model inside the backend instead (see [Model switches and "Claude only"](#model-switches-and-claude-only)).
- **Local answers are slow on a CPU** (about 60 s per answer on a 4-core laptop CPU, mostly reading the prompt) and less reliable than Claude (17/18 vs 18/18). A GPU or Claude is recommended for interactive use.
- **Small knowledge base and evaluation set** (5 articles, 18 questions). Enough to validate the design, not to tune it statistically.
- **English knowledge base.** Questions in other languages work. The answer comes in the question's language, or in the language chosen in the header. The short "not covered" message for off-topic questions stays in English.
- **No authentication for the chat itself.** The admin area is protected by a token, but the chat and the API are open, as on `main`.

---

### 🤖 Claude

All three README files are rewritten and committed locally. Nothing is pushed yet, so I need your go-ahead.

**`main` (the assignment).** I edited it on `dev`, which then goes to `main`, as before.
- **"Try it" (new):** five questions from the evaluation set with what to expect:
  - an answer from one article
  - an answer that combines two articles
  - a German question, answered in German
  - an off-topic question, refused in about 50 ms without calling the model
  - a near-topic question, where the model reads the sources and declines politely

  I tested the two refusal cases on the running system, and that caught a mistake in my first draft, which had them the wrong way round.
- **"Beyond the assignment: optional features" (new):** a short section explaining that `main` is the submission, what `main-features` and `main-mcp` add, and how to try them.
- **Wording:** a note near the top about the branches. In the Plan and the feature list I fixed typos (*"casts"* → *"costs"*, *"hole"* → *"whole"*, *"its"* → *"it's"*) and rewrote the unclear item about clients who insist on an answer. The open items now say where they're built.
- **Unchanged:** the assignment brief, word for word. The architecture, API, decisions and test sections were already complete and correct: the test counts (102 backend, 95% coverage, 17 frontend) match a fresh run.

**`dev-features` and `dev-mcp`.** They now have the order you asked for:
1. **The assignment, as on `main`:** quick start, try it, architecture, API, decisions, testing, configuration, structure, limitations, and AI usage.
2. **"Optional features":** one section with everything added. It starts with an overview table (each feature, where to find it, what it needs), then starting on free ports, the admin tabs, model switches and Claude-only mode, detail and language, follow-up questions, choosing the model by typing, and the CLI. On `dev-mcp` it also covers the MCP server. It ends with the admin API and the feature settings.
3. **The assignment brief**, then the Plan and the feature list with every item ticked.

**Errors fixed along the way on the feature branches:**
- **Test counts:** they said 102 backend / 17 frontend tests. The real numbers are 166 on `dev-features` and 172 on `dev-mcp` (96% coverage), and 35 frontend.
- **API table:** it was missing `GET/PUT /admin/settings`.
- **Out-of-date wording:** some text still called features "planned" that are already built there: More detail, the `language`/`detail` options, the "log-only" history hook, and the decisions table.
- **Links:** every internal link and every link to a file resolves on all three branches.
